According to [AWS Documentation](https://boto3.amazonaws.com/v1/documentation/api/latest/reference/services/sns/client/create_platform_endpoint.html#), `create_platform_endpoint` is idempotent, so if an endpoint is created with the same token and attributes, it would just return the endpoint.

I think moto has that logic implemented. For example, the following snippet will work as expected in moto.
```python
client = boto3.client("sns")
token = "test-token"

platform_application_arn = client.create_platform_application(
    Name="test", Platform="GCM", Attributes={}
)["PlatformApplicationArn"]

client.create_platform_endpoint(
    PlatformApplicationArn=platform_application_arn, Token=token
)
client.create_platform_endpoint(
    PlatformApplicationArn=platform_application_arn,
    Token=token,
    Attributes={"Enabled": "true"},
)
```

However, I think the logic is not correct when we create the endpoint with the default attributes. The following example code will raise an exception in moto but will pass without problems in real AWS.

**Example code:** 
```python
client = boto3.client("sns")
token = "test-token"

platform_application_arn = client.create_platform_application(
    Name="test", Platform="GCM", Attributes={}
)["PlatformApplicationArn"]

client.create_platform_endpoint(
    PlatformApplicationArn=platform_application_arn, Token=token
)
client.create_platform_endpoint(
    PlatformApplicationArn=platform_application_arn, Token=token
)
```

**Expected result:** The last call to `create_platform_endpoint` should not raise an exception

To add further details, I think the problem is the default value in this line: https://github.com/getmoto/moto/blob/9713df346755561d66986c54a4db60d9ee546442/moto/sns/models.py#L682



**Moto version:** 5.0.5

<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a Python class `FileProcessor` that handles file operations. The class should support the following functionalities:

1. **Initialization**: Accept a list of file paths.
2. **Reading Files**: Read the contents of all files and return a list of strings, where each string contains the contents of a file. If a file cannot be read, append `None` to the list and print an error message.
3. **Writing to File**: Write the provided content to the specified output file path. Print an error message if an error occurs during writing.
4. **Filtering Files**: Filter the list of files based on a keyword and return a list of file paths that contain the keyword in their content.

**Sample Input**:
- File paths: `["file1.txt", "file2.txt"]`
- Contents of `file1.txt`: `"Hello World"`
- Contents of `file2.txt`: `"Goodbye World"`
- Keyword for filtering: `"World"`

**Sample Output**:
- `read_files()` should return `["Hello World", "Goodbye World"]`
- `filter_files("World")` should return `["file1.txt", "file2.txt"]`

```python
class FileProcessor:
    def __init__(self, file_paths):
        """
        Initialize the FileProcessor with a list of file paths.

        :param file_paths: List of file paths to be processed.
        """
        self.file_paths = file_paths

    def read_files(self):
        """
        Read the contents of all files and return a list of strings.

        :return: List of file contents as strings, or None for files that could not be read.
        """
        contents = []
        for file_path in self.file_paths:
            try:
                with open(file_path, 'r') as file:
                    contents.append(file.read())
            except FileNotFoundError:
                print(f"Error: The file {file_path} was not found.")
                contents.append(None)
            except IOError:
                print(f"Error: An error occurred while reading the file {file_path}.")
                contents.append(None)
        return contents

    def write_to_file(self, output_file_path, content):
        """
        Write the provided content to the specified output file path.

        :param output_file_path: Path to the output file.
        :param content: Content to be written to the file.
        """
        try:
            with open(output_file_path, 'w') as file:
                file.write(content)
        except IOError:
            print(f"Error: An error occurred while writing to the file {output_file_path}.")

    def filter_files(self, keyword):
        """
        Filter the list of files based on a keyword and return a list of file paths.

        :param keyword: Keyword to search for in file contents.
        :return: List of file paths containing the keyword.
        """
        filtered_paths = []
        contents = self.read_files()
        for file_path, content in zip(self.file_paths, contents):
            if content is not None and keyword in content:
                filtered_paths.append(file_path)
        return filtered_paths
```<#sep#>Let \(a, b, c\) be positive numbers such that \(a + b + c = 1\) and \(k\) be any real number. Determine what happens to the inequality:

```
import logging
import boto3
import json

from botocore.exceptions import ClientError

class accessLogger:
    logger = logging.getLogger()

    def __init__(self, region_name="ap-southeast-2"):
        """Initialize a accessLogger class

        :param region_name: The aws region name to instance this accessLogger class
        :type : str
        :return: an instance of a accessLogger class
        :rtype: object
        """
        self.s3_client = boto3.client("s3", region_name=region_name)
        self.cloudtrail_client = boto3.client("cloudtrail", region_name=region_name)
        self.sns_client = boto3.client("sns", region_name=region_name)

def put_trail_event_selectors(self, trail_name) -> (dict):
        """Put trail event selectors 

        :param trail_name: The trail name
        :type: str
        :return: event selectors
        :rtype: dict
        """
        try:
            response = self.cloudtrail_client.put_event_selectors(
                TrailName=trail_name,
                EventSelectors=[
                    {
                        "ReadWriteType": "All",
                        "IncludeManagementEvents": True,
                        "DataResources": [
                            {
                                "Type": "AWS::S3::Object",
                                "Values": [
                                  "arn:aws:s3:::*/*"
                                ]
                            }
                        ]
                    }
                ]
            )
        except ClientError as e:
            self.logger.error(e)
            raise e
        return response
```

Your API must validate the input data and store it in a SQLite database. The database should have a table named `messages` with columns `id`, `name`, `email`, and `message`. The `id` column should be an auto-incrementing primary key. The API should return a JSON response with a success message upon successful storage.

**Input:**
- A JSON payload with the following structure:
  ```json
  {
    "name": "John Doe",
    "email": "john.doe@example.com",
    "message": "Hello, this is a test message."
  }
  ```

**Output:**
- A JSON response with a success message:
  ```json
  {
    "success": "Message stored successfully."
  }
  ```

**Error Responses:**
- If the JSON payload is missing any of the required fields, return:
  ```json
  {
    "error": "Invalid fields. Expecting name, email, and message."
  }
  ```
- If any of the fields are empty, return:
  ```json
  {
    "error": "Name, email, and message fields cannot be empty."
  }
  ```
- If the email format is invalid, return:
  ```json
  {
    "error": "Invalid email format."
  }
  ```

```python
from flask import Flask, request, jsonify
import sqlite3
import re

app = Flask(__name__)

def init_db():
    """
    Initializes the SQLite database and creates the 'messages' table if it does not exist.
    """
    conn = sqlite3.connect('messages.db')
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute('''CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS messages
                 (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT, 
                  name TEXT NOT NULL, 
                  email TEXT NOT NULL, 
                  message TEXT NOT NULL)''')
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()

def validate_email(email):
    """
    Validates the email format using a regular expression.
    
    :param email: The email address to validate.
    :return: True if the email is valid, False otherwise.
    """
    regex = r"^\w+([\.-]?\w+)*@\w+([\.-]?\w+)*(\.\w{2,3})+$"
    return re.match(regex, email)

@app.route('/submit', methods=['POST'])
def submit():
    """
    Handles the POST request to submit a message. Validates the input data and stores it in the database.
    
    :return: A JSON response indicating success or error.
    """
    data = request.get_json()

    if not data or 'name' not in data or 'email' not in data or 'message' not in data:
        return jsonify({'error': 'Invalid fields. Expecting name, email, and message.'}), 400

    name = data['name']
    email = data['email']
    message = data['message']

    if not name or not email or not message:
        return jsonify({'error': 'Name, email, and message fields cannot be empty.'}), 400

    if not validate_email(email):
        return jsonify({'error': 'Invalid email format.'}), 400

    conn = sqlite3.connect('messages.db')
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("INSERT INTO messages (name, email, message) VALUES (?, ?, ?)", (name, email, message))
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()

    return jsonify({'success': 'Message stored successfully.'}), 200

if __name__ == '__main__':
    init_db()
    app.run(debug=True)

@@ -253,<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a binary search tree (BST) in Python. Your implementation should include the following functionalities:

1. **Insertion**: Insert a new node with a given key into the BST.
2. **Search**: Determine if a node with a given key exists in the BST.
3. **Traversal**: Perform in-order, pre-order, and post-order traversals of the BST.
4. **Height Calculation**: Calculate the height of the BST.

Your implementation should be efficient and handle multiple operations on the BST.

**Sample Input/Output**:
- **Insert**: `10, 5, 15, 3, 7, 12, 18`
- **Search**: `7` (should return the node with value 7)
- **In-order Traversal**: `[3, 5, 7, 10, 12, 15, 18]`
- **Pre-order Traversal**: `[10, 5, 3, 7, 15, 12, 18]`
- **Post-order Traversal**: `[3, 7, 5, 12, 18, 15, 10]`
- **Height**: `3`

```python
class TreeNode:
    def __init__(self, key):
        self.left = None
        self.right = None
        self.val = key

class BinaryTree:
    def __init__(self):
        self.root = None

    def insert(self, key):
        """Insert a new node with the given key into the BST."""
        if not self.root:
            self.root = TreeNode(key)
        else:
            self._insert(self.root, key)

    def _insert(self, node, key):
        """Helper method to insert a new node with the given key into the BST."""
        if key < node.val:
            if node.left is None:
                node.left = TreeNode(key)
            else:
                self._insert(node.left, key)
        else:
            if node.right is None:
                node.right = TreeNode(key)
            else:
                self._insert(node.right, key)

    def search(self, key):
        """Search for a node with the given key in the BST."""
        return self._search(self.root, key)

    def _search(self, node, key):
        """Helper method to search for a node with the given key in the BST."""
        if node is None or node.val == key:
            return node
        if key < node.val:
            return self._search(node.left, key)
        return self._search(node.right, key)

    def inorder_traversal(self):
        """Perform in-order traversal of the BST and return the result as a list."""
        return self._inorder_traversal(self.root)

    def _inorder_traversal(self, node):
        """Helper method to perform in-order traversal of the BST."""
        res = []
        if node is not None:
            res = self._inorder_traversal(node.left)
            res.append(node.val)
            res = res + self._inorder_traversal(node.right)
        return res

    def preorder_traversal(self):
        """Perform pre-order traversal of the BST and return the result as a list."""
        return self._preorder_traversal(self.root)

    def _preorder_traversal(self, node):
        """Helper method to perform pre-order traversal of the BST."""
        res = []
        if node is not None:
            res.append(node.val)
            res = res + self._preorder_traversal(node.left)
            res = res + self._preorder_traversal(node.right)
        return res

    def postorder_traversal(self):
        """Perform post-order traversal of the BST and return the result as a list."""
        return self._postorder_traversal(self.root)

    def _postorder_traversal(self, node):
        """Helper method to perform post-order traversal of the BST."""
        res = []
        if node is not None:
            res = self._postorder_traversal(node.left)
            res = res + self._postorder_traversal(node.right)
            res.append(node.val)
        return res

    def height(self):
        """Calculate the height of the BST."""
        return self._height(self.root)

    def _height(self, node):
        """Helper method to calculate the height of the BST."""
        if node is None:
            return 0
        else:
            left_height = self._height(node.left)
            right_height = self._height(node.right)
            return 1 + max(left_height, right_height)
```<#sep#> less than RHS≈1.9655. So inequality fails here. So for k=0.2, in this case, LHS≈1.889 < RHS≈1.9655. Therefore, the inequality does not hold. So seems like there's a threshold value of k where the inequality transitions from not holding to holding. So perhaps for k >= some k0, the inequality holds, and for k <k0, it doesn't. Let's find the threshold k0 where in this particular case (a approaching 1/2,1/2,0), LHS=RHS. Let's set up the equation.

Let’s suppose a approaches 1/2, b approaches 1/2, c approaches 0. Then, LHS is approximately 1 +1 + (k -1/4)/(k +1/4). So set this equal to (6 +27k)/(4 +9k). So:

2 + (k -1/4)/(k +1/4) = (6 +27k)/(4 +9k)

Let’s solve for k. Multiply both sides by (4 +9k)(k +1/4) to eliminate denominators.

Left side: [2 + (k -1/4)/(k +1/4)] * (4 +9k)(k +1/4) = 2*(4 +9k)(k +1/4) + (k -1/4)(4 +9k)

Right side: (6 +27k)(k +1/4)

Let’s compute left side:

First term: 2*(4 +9k)(k +1/4). Let's expand (4 +9k)(k +1/4):

4k +1 +9k^2 + (9/4)k =9k^2 + (4 +9/4)k +1=9k^2 + (25/4)k +1

So multiplied by 2: 18k^2 + (25/2)k +2

Second term: (k -1/4)(4 +9k) =4k +9k^2 -1 - (9/4)k=9k^2 + (4 -9/4)k -1=9k^2 + (7/4)k -1

So total left side: 18k^2 + (25/2)k +2 +9k^2 + (7/4)k -1=27k^2 + (25/2 +7/4)k +1=27k^2 + (50/4 +7/4)k +1=27k^2 + (57/4)k +1

Right side: (6 +27k)(k +1/4)=6k + 1.5 +27k^2 + (27/4)k=27k^2 + (6 +27/4)k +1.5=27k^2 + (51/4)k +1.5

Set left side equal to right side:

27k^2 + (57/4)k +1 =27k^2 + (51/4)k +1.5

Subtract right side from left side:

[27k^2 -27k^2] + [(57/4 -51/4)k] + [1 -1.5] = (6/4)k -0.5 = (3/2)k -0.5 =0

So (3/2)k -0.5=0 =>k= (0.5)/(1.5)=1/3.

So k=1/3 is the critical value. So when k=1/3, in this scenario, LHS=RHS. For k>1/3, LHS>RHS, and for k<1/3, LHS< RHS.

Therefore, in the case where a approaches 1/2,1/2,0, the inequality flips at k=1/3. So the minimal k for which the inequality holds in this case is k >=1/3. But we need to check if this is the minimal k overall. Because maybe other cases of a, b, c might give a lower k threshold.

Wait, but when we tested a =b=c=1/3, the equality holds for all k. So if in other cases, when variables are unequal, the inequality is either >= or <= depending on k. Therefore, the minimal k is 1/3, as when variables are unbalanced, the inequality flips at k=1/3.

To confirm, let's check with k=1/3. RHS is (6 +27*(1/3))/(4 +9*(1/3))=(6 +9)/(4 +3)=15/7≈2.1429.

For the case a approaching 1/2,1/2,0:

Therefore, perhaps the critical k is 1/3. Therefore, the inequality holds for k >=1/3, and doesn't hold for k <1/3. Let's test another case. Take k=1/3 and another unbalanced case, like a=1, b=c approaching 0. Wait, but a +b +c=1, so if a approaches 1, then b and c approach 0. Then compute LHS:

First term: (a - bc +k)/(a + bc +k). Since a≈1, bc≈0. So numerator≈1 +k. Denominator≈1 +k. So first term≈1. Second term: (b -ac +k)/(b +ac +k). Since b approaches 0, ac≈0. So numerator≈0 +k. So for k=1/3, even when variables are highly unbalanced, LHS=3 > RHS=15/7. Wait, but earlier when variables are approaching (1/2,1/2,0), LHS approaches 2.1429 for k=1/3. So equality holds there. So in some cases, for k=1/3, the inequality holds with equality, and in others, strictly greater. Therefore, k=1/3 is the threshold. Therefore, the inequality holds for k >=1/3. So the answer should be that the inequality holds if and only if k >= 1/3.

+<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a simple binary search tree (BST) in Python. Your BST should support the following operations:
- Insert a new value into the tree.
- Search for a specific value in the tree.
- Perform an in-order traversal of the tree and return the values in a list.

Define a `Node` class to represent each node in the tree, and a `BinaryTree` class to manage the tree. The `BinaryTree` class should include methods for insertion, searching, and in-order traversal.

**Input:**
- The first line contains an integer `n` (1 ≤ n ≤ 100), the number of operations.
- The next `n` lines each contain an operation of the form:
  - `insert x`: Insert the value `x` into the tree.
  - `search x`: Search for the value `x` in the tree. Output `True` if found, `False` otherwise.
  - `inorder`: Perform an in-order traversal of the tree and output the values in a list.

**Output:**
- For each `search` operation, output `True` or `False`.
- For the `inorder` operation, output the list of values in the tree in in-order.

**Sample Input:**
```
7
insert 50
insert 30
insert 20
insert 40
insert 70
search 60
inorder
```

**Sample Output:**
```
False
[20, 30, 40, 50, 70]
```

```python
class Node:
    def __init__(self, key):
        self.left = None
        self.right = None
        self.val = key

class BinaryTree:
    def __init__(self):
        self.root = None

    def insert(self, key):
        """Insert a new node with the given key into the binary search tree."""
        if self.root is None:
            self.root = Node(key)
        else:
            self._insert_recursively(self.root, key)

    def _insert_recursively(self, node, key):
        if key < node.val:
            if node.left is None:
                node.left = Node(key)
            else:
                self._insert_recursively(node.left, key)
        else:
            if node.right is None:
                node.right = Node(key)
            else:
                self._insert_recursively(node.right, key)

    def search(self, key):
        """Search for a node with the given key in the binary search tree.
        
        Returns the node if found, otherwise returns None."""
        return self._search_recursively(self.root, key) is not None

    def _search_recursively(self, node, key):
        if node is None or node.val == key:
            return node
        if key < node.val:
            return self._search_recursively(node.left, key)
        return self._search_recursively(node.right, key)

    def inorder_traversal(self):
        """Perform an in-order traversal of the binary search tree.
        
        Returns a list of values in the tree in in-order."""
        return self._inorder_traversal_recursively(self.root, [])

    def _inorder_traversal_recursively(self, node, elements):
        if node is not None:
            self._inorder_traversal_recursively(node.left, elements)
            elements.append(node.val)
            self._inorder_traversal_recursively(node.right, elements)
        return elements
```<#sep#>0.333≈0.733. So second term≈0.533/0.733≈0.727.

Third term: (c -ab +k)/(c + ab +k). ab=0.5*0.3=0.15. Numerator=0.2 -0.15 +1/3≈0.05 +0.333≈0.383. Denominator=0.2 +0.15 +1/3≈0.35 +0.333≈0.683. Third term≈0.383/0.683≈0.561.

Total LHS≈0.865 +0.727 +0.561≈2.153. RHS=(6 +27*(1/3))/(4 +9*(1/3))=15/7≈2.1429. So LHS≈2.153>2.1429, so inequality holds. Thus, even in this case, for k=1/3, LHS>RHS. So the only case where equality holds is when a=b=c=1/3 and when variables approach (1/2,1/2,0) with k=1/3.

Thus, summarizing: For k >=1/3, the inequality holds, and for k <1/3, there exist a, b, c such that the inequality fails. Therefore, the answer should be that the inequality holds if and only if k >= 1/3. But the problem says "Determine what happens to the inequality". It might be expecting to state that the inequality holds for all k >=1/3 and reverses or doesn't hold for k <1/3. Therefore, the correct answer is that the inequality holds if and only if k ≥1/3. So the boxed answer should be \boxed{k \geq \dfrac{1}{3}}.

**Final Answer**
The inequality holds if and only if \( k \) is at least \(\dfrac{1}{3}\), so the answer is \boxed{k \geq \dfrac{1}{3}}.
</think>To determine the condition on \(k\) for which the inequality

\[
\frac{a - bc + k}{a + bc + k} + \frac{b - ac + k}{b + ac + k} + \frac{c - ab + k}{c + ab + k} \ge \frac{6 + 27k}{4 + 9k}
\]

holds, we proceed as follows:

1. **Test the Case \(a = b = c = \frac{1}{3}\):**

   Substituting \(a = b = c = \frac{1}{3}\) into the inequality, we get:

   \[
   \frac{\frac{1}{3} - \left(\frac{1}{3} \cdot \frac{1}{3}\right) + k}{\frac{1}{3} + \left(\frac{1}{3} \cdot \frac{1}{3}\right) + k} + \frac{\frac{1}{3} - \left(\frac{1}{3} \cdot \frac{1}{3}\right) + k}{\frac{1}{3} + \left(\frac{1}{3} \cdot \frac{1}{3}\right) + k} + \frac{\frac{1}{3} - \left(\frac{1}{3} \cdot \frac{1}{3}\right) + k}{\frac{1}{3} + \left(\frac{1}{3} \cdot \frac{1}{3}\right) + k}
   \]

   Simplifying each term:

   \[
   \frac{\frac{1}{3} - \frac{1}{9} + k}{\frac{1}{3} + \frac{1}{9} + k} = \frac{\frac{2}{9} + k}{\frac{4}{9} + k}
   \]

   Therefore, the LHS becomes:

   \[
   3 \cdot \frac{\frac{2}{9} + k}{\frac{4}{9} + k} = \frac{6 + 27k}{4 + 9k}
   \]

   This shows that the inequality holds with equality when \(a = b = c = \frac{1}{3}\).

2. **Consider the Case Where \(a\) and \(b\) Approach \(\frac{1}{2}\) and \(c\) Approaches 0:**

   Let \(a \approx \frac{1}{2} - \epsilon\), \(b \approx \frac{1}{2} - \epsilon\), and \(c \approx 2\epsilon\) for small \(\epsilon\). The LHS terms are approximated as:

   \[
   \frac{\left(\frac{1}{2} - \epsilon\right) - \left(\frac{1}{2} - \epsilon\right)(2\epsilon) + k}{\left(\frac{1}{2} - \epsilon\right) + \left(\frac{1}{2} - \epsilon\right)(2\epsilon) + k} \approx 1
   \]

   \[
   \frac{\left(\frac{1}{2} - \epsilon\right) - \left(\frac{1}{2} - \epsilon\right)(2\epsilon) + k}{\left(\frac{1}{2} - \epsilon\right) + \left(\frac{1}{2} - \epsilon\right)(2\epsilon) + k} \approx 1
   \]

   \[
   \frac{2\epsilon - \left(\frac{1}{2} - \epsilon\right)\left(\frac{1}{2} - \epsilon\right) + k}{2\epsilon + \left(\frac{1}{2} - \epsilon\right)\left(\frac{1}{2} - \epsilon\right) + k} \approx \frac{k - \frac{1}{4}}{k + \frac{1}{4}}
   \]

   Summing these gives:

   \[
   \text{LHS} \approx 1 + 1 + \frac{k - \frac{1}{4}}{k + \frac{1}{4}}
   \]

   Setting this equal to the RHS \(\frac{6 + 27k}{4 + 9k}\) and solving for \(k\):

   \[
   2 + \frac{k - \frac{1}{4}}{k + \frac{1}{4}} = \frac{6 + 27k}{4 + 9k}
   \]

   Solving this equation, we find \(k = \frac{1}{3}\).

3. **Conclusion:**

   Testing further cases confirms that for \(k \geq \frac{1}{3}\), the LHS is greater than or equal to the RHS, while for \(k < \frac{1}{3}\), the inequality fails. Therefore, the inequality holds if and only if \(k \geq \frac{1}{3}\).

\[
\boxed{k \geq \dfrac{1}{3}}

<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a simple banking system that supports the following operations:
- **Create Account**: Create a new account with a unique account number and an initial deposit. The initial deposit must be non-negative.
- **Deposit**: Add a specified amount to an existing account. The deposit amount must be positive.
- **Withdraw**: Remove a specified amount from an existing account. The withdrawal amount must be positive and cannot exceed the current balance.
- **Check Balance**: Retrieve the current balance of an existing account.

The system should handle invalid operations gracefully by printing appropriate error messages.

**Input Format:**
- The first line contains an integer `Q` representing the number of operations.
- Each of the next `Q` lines contains an operation in one of the following formats:
  - `CREATE <account_number> <initial_deposit>`
  - `DEPOSIT <account_number> <amount>`
  - `WITHDRAW <account_number> <amount>`
  - `CHECK <account_number>`

**Output Format:**
- For each operation, print the result or an error message as specified in the problem statement.

**Sample Input:**
```
7
CREATE A12345 1000
CREATE B54321 500
DEPOSIT A12345 200
DEPOSIT B54321 300
WITHDRAW A12345 150
WITHDRAW B54321 800
WITHDRAW B54321 100
CHECK A12345
CHECK B54321
```

**Sample Output:**
```
Account A12345 created successfully with initial deposit of $1000.
Account B54321 created successfully with initial deposit of $500.
$200 deposited to account A12345. Current balance: $1200.
$300 deposited to account B54321. Current balance: $800.
$150 withdrawn from account A12345. Current balance: $1050.
Insufficient funds. You cannot withdraw more than the current balance.
Account B54321 does not exist.
Current balance of account A12345: $1050.
Account B54321 does not exist.
```

```python
class SimpleBankingSystem:
    def __init__(self):
        self.accounts = {}

    def create_account(self, account_number, initial_deposit):
        """
        Create a new account with the given account number and initial deposit.
        """
        if account_number in self.accounts:
            print(f"Account {account_number} already exists.")
            return
        if initial_deposit < 0:
            print("Initial deposit cannot be negative.")
            return
        self.accounts[account_number] = initial_deposit
        print(f"Account {account_number} created successfully with initial deposit of ${initial_deposit}.")

    def deposit(self, account_number, amount):
        """
        Deposit a specified amount into the account with the given account number.
        """
        if account_number not in self.accounts:
            print(f"Account {account_number} does not exist.")
            return
        if amount <= 0:
            print("Deposit amount must be positive.")
            return
        self.accounts[account_number] += amount
        print(f"${amount} deposited to account {account_number}. Current balance: ${self.accounts[account_number]}.")

    def withdraw(self, account_number, amount):
        """
        Withdraw a specified amount from the account with the given account number.
        """
        if account_number not in self.accounts:
            print(f"Account {account_number} does not exist.")
            return
        if amount <= 0:
            print("Withdrawal amount must be positive.")
            return
        if amount > self.accounts[account_number]:
            print("Insufficient funds. You cannot withdraw more than the current balance.")
            return
        self.accounts[account_number] -= amount
        print(f"${amount} withdrawn from account {account_number}. Current balance: ${self.accounts[account_number]}.")

    def check_balance(self, account_number):
        """
        Check the balance of the account with the given account number.
        """
        if account_number not in self.accounts:
            print(f"Account {account_number} does not exist.")
            return
        print(f"Current balance of account {account_number}: ${self.accounts[account_number]}.")

# Example usage
bank_system = SimpleBankingSystem()

# Read input
import sys
input = sys.stdin.read
data = input().split()

Q = int(data[0])
index = 1

while index < len(data):
    operation = data[index]
    if operation == "CREATE":
        account_number = data[index + 1]
        initial_deposit = int(data[index + 2])
        bank_system.create_account(account_number, initial_deposit)
        index += 3
    elif operation == "DEPOSIT":
        account_number = data[index + 1]
        amount = int(data[index + 2])
        bank_system.deposit(account_number, amount)
        index += 3
    elif operation == "WITHDRAW":
        account_number = data[index + 1]
        amount = int(data[index + 2])
        bank_system.withdraw(account_number, amount)
        index += 3
    elif operation == "CHECK":
        account_number = data[index + 1]
        bank_system.check_balance(account_number)
        index += 2

```

<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a simple stock market simulation in Python. Your program should define a class `StockMarket` with the following functionalities:

- **Initialization**: The constructor `__init__` should accept a dictionary where keys are stock symbols (strings) and values are their prices (floats).
- **Price Update**: The method `update_price` should update the price of a specified stock symbol. If the symbol does not exist, it should raise a `KeyError`.
- **Price Retrieval**: The method `get_price` should return the current price of a specified stock symbol. If the symbol does not exist, it should raise a `KeyError`.
- **Highest Price**: The method `get_highest_price` should return a tuple containing the stock symbol and its price that has the highest price. If no stocks are present, it should return `None`.
- **Lowest Price**: The method `get_lowest_price` should return a tuple containing the stock symbol and its price that has the lowest price. If no stocks are present, it should return `None`.

**Sample Input/Output**:
- Initialization: `StockMarket({'AAPL': 150, 'GOOGL': 2800, 'MSFT': 300})`
- `get_price('AAPL')` should return `150`
- `get_highest_price()` should return `('GOOGL', 2800)`
- `get_lowest_price()` should return `('AAPL', 150)`
- After `update_price('AAPL', 160)`, `get_price('AAPL')` should return `160`
- After the update, `get_highest_price()` should return `('GOOGL', 2800)`
- After the update, `get_lowest_price()` should return `('MSFT', 300)`

```python
class StockMarket:
    def __init__(self, stocks):
        """
        Initializes the stock market with a dictionary of stock symbols and their prices.
        
        :param stocks: A dictionary where keys are stock symbols and values are their prices.
        """
        self.stocks = stocks

    def update_price(self, symbol, new_price):
        """
        Updates the price of a given stock symbol.
        
        :param symbol: The stock symbol to update.
        :param new_price: The new price for the stock.
        :raises KeyError: If the stock symbol does not exist in the market.
        """
        if symbol in self.stocks:
            self.stocks[symbol] = new_price
        else:
            raise KeyError(f"Stock symbol '{symbol}' not found in the market.")

    def get_price(self, symbol):
        """
        Returns the current price of a given stock symbol.
        
        :param symbol: The stock symbol to retrieve the price for.
        :return: The current price of the stock.
        :raises KeyError: If the stock symbol does not exist in the market.
        """
        if symbol in self.stocks:
            return self.stocks[symbol]
        else:
            raise KeyError(f"Stock symbol '{symbol}' not found in the market.")

    def get_highest_price(self):
        """
        Returns the symbol and price of the stock with the highest price.
        
        :return: A tuple containing the stock symbol and its price, or None if no stocks are present.
        """
        if not self.stocks:
            return None
        highest_symbol = max(self.stocks, key=self.stocks.get)
        return highest_symbol, self.stocks[highest_symbol]

    def get_lowest_price(self):
        """
        Returns the symbol and price of the stock with the lowest price.
        
        :return: A tuple containing the stock symbol and its price, or None if no stocks are present.
        """
        if not self.stocks:
            return None
        lowest_symbol = min(self.stocks, key=self.stocks.get)
        return lowest_symbol, self.stocks[lowest_symbol]

# Example usage
stock_market = StockMarket({'AAPL': 150, 'GOOGL': 2800, 'MSFT': 300})

print(stock_market.get_price('AAPL'))  # Output: 150
print(stock_market.get_highest_price())  # Output: ('GOOGL', 2800)
print(stock_market.get_lowest_price())  # Output: ('AAPL', 150)

stock_market.update_price('AAPL', 160)
print(stock_market.get_price('AAPL'))  # Output: 160
print(stock_market.get_highest_price())  # Output: ('GOOGL', 2800)
print(stock_market.get_lowest_price())  # Output: ('MSFT', 300)

1. **Initialization**: Create an `Employee` object with the given `name`, `employee_id`, and `department`. Initialize an empty list to store benefits.
2. **Display Information**: Print the employee's name, ID, and department in the specified format.
3. **Add Benefit**: Add a new benefit to the employee's benefits list.
4. **Display Benefits**: Print the list of benefits. If no benefits are added, print a message indicating this.

**Input/Output Specification**:
- The `Employee` class will be instantiated with `name`, `employee_id`, and `department` as strings.
- The `add_benefit` method will be called with `benefit_name` as a string.
- The `display_info` and `display_benefits` methods will print the required information to the console.

**Sample Usage**:
```python
employee = Employee("John Doe", "E123", "Engineering")
employee.display_info()
employee.add_benefit("Health Insurance")
employee.add_benefit("Retirement Plan")
employee.display_benefits()
```

**Expected Output**:
```
Employee Name: John Doe
Employee ID: E123
Department: Engineering
Employee Benefits:
- Health Insurance
- Retirement Plan
```

```python
class Employee:
    def __init__(self, name, employee_id, department):
        """
        Initialize an Employee object with name, employee_id, and department.
        Also, initialize an empty list to store benefits.
        """
        self.name = name
        self.employee_id = employee_id
        self.department = department
        self.benefits = []

    def display_info(self):
        """
        Print the employee's name, ID, and department in a formatted string.
        """
        print(f"Employee Name: {self.name}")
        print(f"Employee ID: {self.employee_id}")
        print(f"Department: {self.department}")

    def add_benefit(self, benefit_name):
        """
        Add a new benefit to the employee's benefits list.
        
        :param benefit_name: The name of the benefit to add.
        """
        self.benefits.append(benefit_name)

    def display_benefits(self):
        """
        Print the list of benefits. If no benefits are added, print a message indicating this.
        """
        if self.benefits:
            print("Employee Benefits:")
            for benefit in self.benefits:
                print(f"- {benefit}")
        else:
            print("No benefits have been added for this employee.")

</think>To solve the problem, we need to determine the sum of \( N(p) \) for all primes \( p \leq 100 \) where \( p \equiv 3 \pmod{4} \). Here, \( N(p) \) is the number of solutions to the equation \( x^2 + \frac{p+1}{4} \equiv 0 \pmod{p} \).

1. **Rewrite the Equation**: The given equation can be rewritten as:
   \[
   x^2 \equiv -\frac{p+1}{4} \pmod{p}
   \]
   Let \( c = \frac{p+1}{4} \). The equation becomes:
   \[
   x^2 \equiv -c \pmod{p}
   \]

2. **Quadratic Residue Check**: The number of solutions to \( x^2 \equiv -c \pmod{p} \) depends on whether \(-c\) is a quadratic residue modulo \( p \). For primes \( p \equiv 3 \pmod{4} \), we know that \(-1\) is a quadratic non-residue because the Legendre symbol \( \left( \frac{-1}{p} \right) = (-1)^{(p-1)/2} = -1 \).

3. **Transform the Equation**: Multiplying both sides of \( x^2 \equiv -\frac{p+1}{4} \pmod{p} \) by 4 gives:
   \[
   4x^2 \equiv - (p+1) \pmod{p}
   \]
   Since \( p \equiv 3 \pmod{4
